The 327 `inbounds nuw` GEPs of the corpus will show whether this matters. | open | docstring of `MojoProof/Sem/Eval.lean` |

| B13 | soundness gap (TRUST T12) | high | `!invariant.load`, `willreturn`, `nosync`, `nofree`, `norecurse`, `memory(...)` and non-`byval` `nonnull` added undefined behavior that the semantics did not check. | fixed | `norecurse` recursion is undefined behavior; `nonnull` makes a null pointer poison (`withAttrs`); `!invariant.load` is recorded in the trace and `Grid.Sound` includes `Grid.Kept`; the other attributes are refused on defined functions (`Func.check`); `trace_examples`; tests "invariant load written", "norecurse recursion", "willreturn", "nonnull null"; every small kernel's and `vector_addition`'s run keeps `!invariant.load` |

Supporting mgemm needs a model of pending copies. | partly fixed (b92a2bb): `MojoProof/Sem/Async.lean` models pending copies, `commit_group` and `wait_group n` (`Async.wait_run`, `Async.visible`, `Async.visible_bytes`); the grid does not use it yet (B1048); 131ef30: the completion is the ISA's (`Async.Step`, `Async.Run`: any copy may land at any time; B1074); 13853c6: tests/gpu/cp-async/record.txt, one A100 run agreeing with the lazy run; 6a4bcee: every run of the model gives the words it reads after either wait (`wait1_bytes`, `wait0_bytes`) | MojoProofTest/Async.lean; MojoProofTest/AsyncGpu.lean |
| B15 | grid layer (`MojoProof/Sem/Grid.lean`) | gap (semantics) | `ldmatrix` and `mma` have no grid semantics (a run reaching one stops as unsupported). Determinism of race-free runs is not yet proved. | partly fixed (27616ec, da8abc0, a1f6c97, ab33fea): `MojoProof/Sem/TensorCore.lean` gives `ldmatrix` and `mma` their semantics as warp-collective steps (`ldmatrix_gather`, `mmaStep_gather`, `mma_exact`, `mma_contract`) with the obligation `Grid.warpReady`; `Grid.fire` runs `mma` for a whole warp with bf16 or f16 inputs and an f32 accumulator (`Grid.fire_mma`, ab33fea); `ldmatrix` is still unsupported (B1325). The barrier with a returned thread and determinism are unchanged: open | `Grid.fire`; MojoProofTest/TensorCore.lean, MojoProofTest/TensorCoreGrid.lean |
| B16 | `rsqrt.approx` fallback (`MojoProof.Sem.rsqrt`) | gap (layer norm) | When the oracle's word misses the contract, `rsqrt` falls back to a FloatLib word. Before 5fff152 the fallback was `1 / sqrt x`, which met the contract only for positive normal `x` (`rsqrtOk_div_sqrt`), so NaN, zero, subnormal, negative and infinite operands had no proved word | fixed (5fff152) | the fallback is FloatLib's `Model.rSqrt`; `rsqrtOk_rSqrt` (MojoProof/Sem/Float.lean:319) proves it meets `rsqrtOk` on every operand (`toReal_rSqrt_eq_roundAt`, `rSqrt_of_isZero`) and replaces `rsqrtOk_div_sqrt`, so every value of `rsqrt` meets the contract (`rsqrtOk_of_rsqrt`); since 3241315 `rsqrt` returns a word, not an option (B350) |
| B17 | `MojoProof.Sem.scalarOf` | low | A load of a scalar whose stored bits exceed its width gave `undef`; LangRef ("load") makes a nonzero padding bit poison. | fixed | the result is poison (the whole scalar, coarser than LangRef's byte) |
| B18 | `MojoProof.Sem.Grid.fire` | medium | A barrier number outside 0-15 completed; PTX ISA 9.5, section 9.7.15.1, has 16 barriers per block. | fixed | it is undefined behavior |
| B19 | `MojoProof.Sem.Layout.parse` | high | Unknown or unimplemented data layout specifications were skipped: `E` was recorded but values stayed little-endian, `a:N` was ignored, and a pointer whose index width is below its size wrapped GEP offsets at the index width but checked `nusw` / `nuw` at the pointer width (LangRef: the offset wraps in the low index-width bits and leaves the others). | fixed | `parse` returns an error naming the first unimplemented specification and the importer refuses the module; the index width is the pointer size; refusal tests for `E`, `p:64:64:64:32`, `a:64` |
| B20 | memory model (`MojoProof.Sem.Grid.Step`) | high (open) | Steps interleave over one memory and `atomicrmw` ignored its ordering and scope: two `monotonic` exchanges and adds on two locations can both read 0 under LLVM, which no interleaving gives. | fixed in the statements: atomics other than `seq_cst` without `syncscope` are refused (the corpus has one atomic, `seq_cst`); the memory records every access (byte range, thread, read or write, atomic or not) and every barrier completion in a ghost trace, and `Grid.Sound` includes race freedom under scoped happens-before (`Grid.RaceFree`), so a sound grid's runs are LLVM's (TRUST T13). Open: a race-freedom proof for a real kernel (vector addition). The full `tiled_matmul` launch's trace (about 600,000 accesses, too long for the quadratic `racy`) is checked by `fast` (`MojoProof/Sem/Race.lean`, `fast_sound`: `fast tr = false → racy tr = false`, for every trace) | `exec` refuses the other atomics; `trace_examples`; tests "race in a block", "barrier in a block", "race across blocks", "shuffle in a warp" (a shuffle orders no access, by design), "atomics"; every small kernel's, `vector_addition`'s and one block of `tiled_matmul`'s run has a race-free trace |
| B21 | `nsz` (`MojoProof.Sem.withFlags`, `maxnum`) | high | `nsz` flipped the sign of a zero result; LangRef lets it flip zero operands, so `fdiv nsz 1.0, -0.0` may be either infinity. `fneg` ignored it, and `maxnum nsz (+0, -0)` could not give `-0` (LangRef: either operand). | fixed by refusal: `nsz` on any instruction or call is unsupported (`FastMath.check`; the corpus has none), `maxnum` orders `-0` below `+0` as LangRef and PTX's `max` do; open gap: `nsz`'s operand-sign choices | `floatBin`, `fnegV`, `fcmpV` call `FastMath.check` |
| B22 | call flags (`MojoProof.Sem.exec`, `intrinsic`) | high | `rsqrt.approx` ignored its call's `nnan` / `ninf` (a NaN or infinity instead of poison), and calls to defined functions dropped their flags. | fixed | `nnan` / `ninf` on `maxnum`, `minnum`, `rsqrt.approx` give poison as `withFlags` says; any other flag on a call is unsupported |
| B23 | `noundef` (`MojoProof.Sem.exec`, `launchArgs`, `Grid.fire`) | high | Only the callee's `noundef` parameters were checked: a call-site `noundef` on a poison argument, a kernel's `noundef` parameter at the launch, a load with `!noundef` and the results of collective calls (which bypassed `retAttrs`) were not. | fixed | each is undefined behavior when the value is not `defined`; a waiting thread keeps its callee's return attributes and `resumeAll` applies them; call-site attributes other than `noundef` and metadata other than `!invariant.load` / `!noundef` are unsupported (the corpus has none) |
| B24 | memory ownership (`MojoProof.Sem.Mem.check`) | high | Blocks had no owner and accesses no accessor: a thread could publish a pointer to its live `alloca`, synchronize, and another thread read it, although PTX makes local memory private to each thread (PTX ISA 9.5, 5.1.5); the same held for another block's shared memory (5.1.7). | fixed | a block has an `Owner` (every thread, one block of threads, one thread), every access names its thread (`Who`), and an access the owner does not admit is undefined behavior; `lifetime` markers on memory the thread does not own are unsupported; test "another thread's alloca" (and "own alloca" as the control) |
| B25 | `byval` kernel arguments (`MojoProof.Sem.Grid.start`, `launchArgs`) | high | `launchArgs` ran once per launch, so all threads shared each `byval` copy, writable unless `readonly`: thread 0's write was visible to thread 1 after a barrier. The Grid docstring said the copies were always read-only. | fixed | each thread gets its own copies, owned by it (oracle numbers `Nat.pair 0 (Nat.pair n i)`), read-only exactly when the parameter is `readonly`; test "byval copy per thread" |
| B26 | `mojo_import` (`MojoProof/LLVM/Quote.lean`) | low (test gap) | That the command refuses a corpus file whose SHA-256 is not its manifest row's had no end-to-end test: every corpus file matches its row (the gate's corpus step), and a test needs a second, mismatching manifest. | fixed | the option `mojo_import.root` names the directory of the files and the manifest; `tests/stale/k.ll` was changed after its row in `tests/stale/MANIFEST.tsv` was written, and importing it gives "the file changed" (`MojoProofTest/Import.lean`, `#guard_msgs`; altered artifact bytes) |
| B27 | `shfl.sync` (`MojoProof.Sem.Grid.fire`) | medium | Partial member masks were accepted, a returned lane counted as giving poison, and a lane waiting at another `shfl.sync` (other position, mode or mask) only delayed completion: partial participation and divergence had a semantics that PTX does not give them. | fixed by refusal: the mask must be all 32 bits, every lane of the warp must wait at the same instruction with the same mode and mask; another mask, a returned lane, a lane at another collective call are unsupported, with the reason. Open: lanes are matched on position, not position plus a generation count; under whole-warp participation every completion includes every lane, so their counts agree, but nothing proves it | tests "shuffle" (`bfly` of two lanes gives [1, 0]), "partial shuffle mask", "shuffle with a returned lane", "divergent shuffles" |
| B28 | lexer and parser (`MojoProof.LLVM.Lexer.lexName`, `parse`) | high | `%0` and `%"0"` lexed to the same name string, although LLVM tells the unnamed `%0` from the named `%"0"`: in `define void @f(i32 %0, i32 %"0")` both parameters were `"0"` and `Thread.start` bound both to the first argument; `printDiff`, on the same lexer, could not see it. More generally no name was checked to be defined once. | fixed | the lexer refuses a quoted all-digit name; `parse` refuses a name defined twice (`Module.twice`: `@`, `#n`, `!n`, `!name`, and `%` within a function); refusal tests `%"0"`, `@"1"`, `%a` twice, label `0:` after parameter `%0`, `@f` twice (function and global), `!0` twice |
| B29 | `withAttrs` (`MojoProof/Sem/Thread.lean`) | high | `range` was applied to integer scalars only; on a vector it was ignored, so `<2 x i32> noundef range(i32 0, 1)` accepted `<0, 2>`. | fixed | `range`, `nonnull`, `align` apply element by element to vectors (LangRef: "applies element-wise"); a `range` of another width, an empty range other than `0, 0` and `range` on a non-integer are unsupported; tests "vector range" and its control |
| B30 | `withAttrs` | high | `nonnull` recognized only the null block: a pointer into a block whose address is 0 passed, although LangRef defines `nonnull` by the address bits. | fixed | `nonnull` and `align n` read the address (`Mem.addr`, in the parameter type's space); tests "nonnull at address 0", "align 64 at address 16" and controls |
| B31 | intrinsic declarations (`Func.check`, `exec`) | high | The attributes of an intrinsic declaration were not checked: `declare void @llvm.lifetime.start.p0(ptr noundef align 16)` was accepted and its `align` not enforced; T12 called the declarations' promises met by construction without a list. | fixed (whitelist incomplete: B41) | `Func.check` runs on the declaration at each call with a whitelist of declaration attributes (TRUST T12 names the trusted ones); `align` is enforced; an `immarg` argument must be a constant; tests "intrinsic align" (and control), "intrinsic dereferenceable", "intrinsic function attribute", "immarg" (and control) |
| B32 | `shared` (`MojoProof/Sem/Grid.lean`) | high | A `constant` shared global was allocated writable. | fixed | an `internal constant` shared global is read-only (a store is undefined behavior); an `external constant` one (it would name the writable dynamic shared memory) is unsupported; tests "constant shared global" (and control), "external constant shared global" |
| B33 | metadata (`Stmt.checkMd`, `enter`, the parser) | medium | `phi`s were evaluated in `enter` without looking at their metadata, so `!noundef` on a phi was ignored; a terminator's attachments were dropped by the parser. | fixed | `Stmt.checkMd` (metadata only `!invariant.load` and `!noundef` on a load) runs in `exec` and on every phi in `enter`; the parser refuses attachments on a terminator; tests "phi metadata" (and control), refusal `br ..., !llvm.loop !0` |
| B34 | TRUST T4 (`mojo_import`) | medium (documentation) | T4 called the step from the importer's program to the Lean term untrusted; the check is `evalConst` and an executable comparison, not a kernel-checked equality. | fixed (documented) | T4 and the `Quote.lean` docstring list Lean's compiled evaluation and the comparison code as trusted for that step; a kernel-checked certificate is open |
| B35 | `Grid.fire` docstring | low | It said a participant waiting elsewhere gives `none`; the code faults (undefined behavior at a barrier, unsupported at a shuffle). | fixed (incomplete: B42) | docstring |
| B36 | determinism (`Kernel.Sound`, `Grid.Determinate`) | gap (proof) | `Kernel.Sound` assumes determinacy; it is not proved. Race freedom does not give it: two `seq_cst` `atomicrmw xchg` of different values to one location do not race (`Access.conflict`) but leave different final values, so the confluence theorem must treat atomics (commuting operations only, or a premise). `xchg` is now unsupported (B38); two admitted atomics with different operations or sizes on one location now race (B38). `Kernel.Sound` allows any admissibility predicate and does not include launch validity or argument well-formedness: each certificate must state them. A `shfl.sync` whose source lane is past the block gets poison; a partial-warp proof must account for it. Determinacy is now proved from one run: `Grid.confluent` (T2, abd0233; atomics by `RmwOp.commutes` and `rmw_comm`) and `Grid.start_sound` (MojoProof/Sem/Determinism.lean) give every field of `Grid.Sound`, `determinate` included, from one run to a grid in which every thread has returned that is not bad, for every program and every observation that reads only the blocks; so a `Kernel.Sound` certificate needs one such run per admitted choice and oracle. The notes on what `Kernel.Sound` does not include stand. | fixed (determinacy; the notes stand) | MojoProofTest/Determinism.lean: `sound2` and `final2` (two threads' `alloca` and store: every done grid reachable observes the bytes of 7), twins `racy_twin` (a done grid with a racy trace: sound for no observation) and `trace_twin` (an observation of the trace, which two schedules make differ: sound for no relation) |
| B37 | `Access.overlaps` (`MojoProof/Sem/Spec.lean`) | low (spec) | Its docstring says two accesses touch a common byte, but it compared intervals only: an access of no bytes (a store of an empty structure) strictly inside another overlapped it, so `racy` and `kept` saw a race or a broken `!invariant.load` promise on no byte. Found while proving `fast_sound` (a race must share a byte). | fixed | `overlaps` requires both lengths positive; `trace_examples` has a zero-length write inside a 4-byte write that does not race |
| B38 | atomics (`Stmt.checkAtomic`, `rmw`, `exec`) | gap (semantics) | `atomicrmw` took every operation, float ones with an oracle choice, and its result could be used, so the order of two atomics on one location could change the memory or a register. Now only integer `add`, `and`, `or`, `xor`, `max`, `min`, `umax`, `umin` with an unused result are admitted; float atomics, `xchg`, `sub`, `nand` and used results are unsupported, each with its reason. `racy` exempted every pair of atomic accesses, but `add` and `or` (or two sizes) on one location do not commute. Now each access records the operation of its `atomicrmw` (`Access.atomic : Option RmwOp`), and `Access.conflict` exempts two atomic accesses only if they have the same operation, pointer and size; the width of an admitted `atomicrmw` must be a power of two of at least 8 (LangRef, `atomicrmw`, Arguments), so the size determines the type (an `i31` and an `i32` `add` on one word would not commute). This is stricter than LangRef's data race (atomics never race there), so race freedom here still implies LLVM's. The commutation lemma (two exempt updates in either order give the same memory) is T2's L10: its part about values is `rmw_comm` (MojoProof/Sem/Rmw.lean, every operation in `RmwOp.commutes`'s list, every width, all scalars); its part about memory is `Mem.update_comm` (MojoProof/Sem/Confluence.lean: two updates of one operation at one pointer and type `.int n`, `0 < n`, that each succeed from a memory succeed in both orders and give the same blocks, `next` and trace up to swaps), through `Mem.load_store` (a load after a store by any thread whose check succeeds), `rmw_comm_of_ok` (for all operands, since an operand need not be a scalar of the width), `Mem.load_scalar` (the loaded value is one), `rmw_scalar` and `Layout.scalar_round` (a scalar of a positive width round-trips through its bytes); in the diamond, `Grid.advance_atomic_comm`, and `Grid.advance_comm` now takes that no two pending accesses conflict. | fixed | `Stmt.checkAtomic` at each `atomicrmw`; `rmw` takes no oracle number; `trace_examples` (two `add`s do not race, an `or` and an `add` race, two sizes race); Eval `atomics` (three adds give 6), `atomics, twice` (two adds per thread give 12), `atomics of two operations` (an `add` then an `or` per thread: data race), `atomic i24`, `atomic xchg`, `atomic sub`, `float atomic`, `atomic result used`; `atomicTests` on `tests/corpus/reduction/` (admitted) and its `xchg` and used-result twins (refused); MojoProofTest/Atomics.lean: `rmw_comm` on `umax`, poison and undef, and the twin `add_or` (an `add` and an `or` give 3 and 2); MojoProofTest/Confluence.lean `update_comm` (two `add`s on one word commute in memory), `atomic_comm` and `atomic_diamond` (two threads' `atomicrmw add`s at one word in one grid: the writes overlap, do not conflict, and both orders give the same grid) |
| B39 | allocation numbers (`Mem.alloc`, `Mem.fresh`, `exec`) | gap (semantics) | An `alloca` took the next number of one counter in `Mem`, which every thread's `alloca` increased, so the block numbers depended on the order of the threads' steps: two schedules reached memories equal only up to a renaming of blocks, and the commutation of two threads' steps (T2′) needed that renaming. Now an `alloca` at step `k` of thread `n` makes block `m.next + Nat.pair n k` (`Mem.fresh`), and no step changes `m.next`. Open: `Mem.compact` tabulates the blocks below `next`, so `alloca` blocks are not compacted (performance); the numbers were fresh only if no pointer of the launch named them and no block was there, which the start did not ensure (B46: the "performance only" qualification was premature). | fixed with B46 | Eval `alloca numbers`: two threads publish their `alloca`s, run in both orders (`runText` with `rev`); the numbers are equal across orders and differ between threads. With the old numbering restored the test fails |
| B40 | allocation addresses (`Ctx.addr`, `exec`, `Grid.start`) | gap (semantics) | An `alloca` read its two addresses from the step's oracle numbers 0 and 1, the numbers float operations read their NaN, contraction, `maxnum` and `rsqrt` choices from. A statement that varies the float oracle and keeps the addresses (T3) could not be stated on the oracle: varying it moved `alloca` addresses, which `ptrtoint` and every alignment check read. Now `Ctx` has skeleton choices `addr`, which `Grid.start` takes from `ch` (`ch (Nat.pair 1 (Nat.pair n j))` for thread `n`; shared memory moved to `Nat.pair (b + 2) i`), and an `alloca` at step `k` reads `addr (Nat.pair k 0)` and `addr (Nat.pair k 1)`; the oracle holds float choices only. | fixed | Eval `alloca addresses`: the `ptrtoint` of an `alloca` is the same under oracles 0 and 7 and changes when `ch` does. With the old addresses restored both checks fail |
| B41 | intrinsic declarations (`Func.check`, `Intrinsic.effects`, `exec`) | high | B31's whitelist took `nosync`, `willreturn`, `speculatable`, `memory(...)` and `nocreateundeforpoison` on every intrinsic declaration, so `declare void @llvm.nvvm.barrier.cta.sync.aligned.all(i32) nosync` was accepted although the barrier synchronizes (LangRef `nosync`: undefined behavior; PTX ISA 9.5, 9.7.15.1), and `memory(none)` on the barrier or `memory(argmem: read)` on a lifetime marker was accepted. | fixed | `Intrinsic.effects` says what each member does; `Func.check` takes the member and accepts a promise only if the member keeps it (TRUST T12); `memAccess` reads `memory(...)` by LangRef's location and access kinds; the barrier and a shuffle must be `convergent`; a `nocreateundeforpoison` call with defined arguments and an undefined result is undefined behavior. The cited contract (PTX ISA 9.5, section 9.7.10.28.3.3: at most the `n` most recent groups are pending) says which copies have landed after a wait, not that the others have not: the hardware may land a pending copy at any time. | B1155 | `MojoProof.Sem.AccOk` (now `AccBy`'s case), `mma_contract`, `castDot_ok`, docs/TRUST.md T14; tests/gpu/mma/record.txt; MojoProof/Sem/TensorCoreAcc.lean | medium (trust item) | The A100's `mma.m16n8k16.row.col.f32.bf16.bf16.f32` does not meet `AccOk (dotBound .binary32 16)`, the bound `castDot_ok` proves of a cast-then-`dotLoop` accumulator. Launch 7 (kind 3) has a D word at cell (11, 0), lane 12 register 2, 954914944, which is 18.937 u T from the exact sum (u = 2^-24), 1.113 times the bound. Of the 939 finite D words of the launches of kinds 1 to 5, the other 938 are within it, and on the two exact launches (kind 0) all 256 words are `bfAcc`'s. The alignment probe is consistent with each addend truncated to a multiple of 2^-24 times the largest addend's leading power of two and the sum truncated toward zero. So a caller who instantiates `mma_contract` with `dotBound .binary32 16` for this GPU has a false premise. No kernel theorem is affected: the grid does not dispatch `mma` (B1048). 73be3f7 and 86a3477: the accumulation is modelled over FloatLib's values by `fuseAcc fi fc n p`, which takes blocks of `n` products in order, with C in the first block; each addend is truncated toward zero to `2^(E - p)`, with `E` the largest addend's exponent and exponents raised to emin, and the sum is rounded toward zero by `roundRat`; between the largest finite magnitude and `2^(emax + 1)` the result is that magnitude, and from there on an infinity. `fuseAcc_ok` proves that it meets `AccBy` with the magnitudes `wt a · wt b` and `|c| + 2^emin` and the bound `fuseBound fc n p ⌈K / n⌉`, for every format, block size, width and count. On the A100 all 2,560 D words of 20 launches (cancellation, wide exponent spreads, subnormal and mixed products, near-overflow sums, an alignment probe and an overflow-threshold probe) are `fuseAcc .bfloat16 .binary32 8 24`'s, bit for bit, the cell-(11, 0) word among them; that word is still 1.113 times `dotBound .binary32 16`, so `AccOk (dotBound …)` stays false for this GPU and `fuse16_ok` is the contract to use. `mma_contract` now takes `AccBy`. | fixed (73be3f7, 86a3477) | MojoProofTest/TensorCore.lean `fuse16_ok`, `fuse8_ok`, `contractFuse`, `contract8`, the `fuseRound` guards (ef3aa01); `lake env lean --run tests/gpu/mma/Compare.lean tests/gpu/mma/dump.txt 20` (exit 0, "20 launches: 0 words differ from fuseAcc's, 0 kind-0 words differ from bfAcc's, 1 finite words outside dotBound, 0 words unwritten") |

A row lists the pairs of definitions it exempts and the fingerprint of the reviewed statement (`stmtHash`, the hash of its type, which binder names do not change); a theorem is counted unless every pair its statement asserts is exempted by a row that holds (`lawSplit`), and a row whose theorem's statement or pairs differ is stale and printed with the current fingerprint and pairs. The old rule fails it (scratch mutant, exit 1, logs/s47m-audmut.log) |

| B1414 | GPUProof/Race.lean:634 `racy`, :233 `scan`, :205 `Scan.step`, :193 `Scan.races`; GPUProof/Chunks.lean `racy_by_chunks`; docs/proofs/core.md "`racy` in the kernel", "Fewer pairs" (:570 `racy`, :214 `scan` at ab56042) | medium (cost) | `decide +kernel` on `racy` costs about the square of the events: 170 s and 16 GB at 192 events, out of memory at 24 GB at `vector_addition`'s 684. The cost is the pair loop (`tr[i]?` and `Access.conflict` per pair), not happens-before. The clock pass (5bebfc7) is the body of `racy`, `hb` and `ordered`, with `racy_iff`, `hb_iff` and `ordered_iff` unchanged: 1.4 to 6 times less kernel time and up to 3 times less memory on every trace but the largest hand-over (6.7 s at 96 events, 27 s and 5.0 GB at 192, 116 s and 16 GB at 384), and still out of memory at 24 GB at the 684-event launch (exit 143; the journal records the scope's `oom-kill`). The pass over fewer pairs (09902c6) is the body of `racy`, with `racy_iff` unchanged. Each access is checked against the earlier writes and, when it writes, the earlier others, with its bytes built once: of the launch's 233,586 pairs, 63,350 are visited. On the traces over 4 s that is 7 to 28% less kernel time than the clock pass: 4.9 s at 96 events, 24 s and 4.3 GB at 192, 90 s and 13 GB at 384. The toy launch at 684 events is still out of memory at 24 GB (exit 143; oom-kill in the journal after 3 min 28 s). Through `sig` the launch is kernel-checked in twelve chained proofs (270 s of kernel time, 9.3 GB, axioms propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound) and is out of memory in one proof (2 min 21 s). The cost left is the byte comparisons, 1.6 million at the launch. | fixed in part (09902c6; 2a531bd): the reduction in candidate pairs is proved (`racy`'s body, `racy_iff` unchanged). The chunked certificate is maintained automation: `racy_by_chunks m` (GPUProof/Chunks.lean) proves `racy S tr = false` for every machine with `ToExpr` instances, the evaluator proposing the state after every `m` positions and the kernel checking each chunk in a lemma of its own (`scan_add`, `scan_chunk`); `trace_literal%` writes the evaluator's trace out as a literal. Tested on the toy ring at 11 and 37 threads with two `#guard_msgs` failures, and on vector_addition's launch through `sig` (MojoProofTest/RacySig.lean, 12 chunks, 283 s of kernel time, 4:43-5:47 in `lake build`, 8.5 GB). The finding-3 part is fixed (b7d533c): `MojoProofTest.RacySig.trSig_run` (MojoProofTest/RacySigRun.lean) proves by `decide +kernel` that the literal trace `trSig` is the run's trace through `sig`, and `vadd_run_racy` that the run succeeds and its trace through `sig` is race free, at one program (vector_addition) at one launch (91-92 s and 15.58 GB in `lake build`, B1398); `vaddTrace` is the run of `gridF32` from `startF32` (B1430). The finding-5 part is fixed (73ab66f): the chunk helpers' docstrings are exact (B1378). The interval part is fixed (66c9670): on a machine that proves its placement injective (`Sig.placeInj`, which gives the law `Place`), the pass decides whether two accesses share a byte by their buffers and intervals of offsets (`Sig.share`, `Sig.share_iff`, `Access.meets_iff`), with `racy_iff` unchanged for every machine; a machine without it compares bytes. `vadd_sig_racy` is one proof by `decide +kernel` (63.7 s of kernel time, 9.1 GB, where it ran out of memory at 24 GB); by chunks it takes 77 s, where it took 266 s. open: the pair count still grows with the square of the accesses (one block of `tiled_matmul` has 37,888), and a machine without `placeInj` still compares bytes. The clock pass in 5bebfc7; measured again (f03e97d, core.md "Checking the bytes first, measured and not taken"): bytes first cuts `vadd_sig_racy` from 45.3 s to 25.1 s but makes the small tests 1.13 to 1.68 times slower, and a buffer precheck gives 37.6 s at 1.2 to 1.22 times slower; neither is taken. The visit of the pairs sorted by `Sig.key` was measured and not taken (e01d737, core.md): 1.7 times faster at the 684-event launch, 1.19 to 13.2 times slower on the small tests; the kernel's sort alone exceeds the rule on `twoBufs 7` | Toy.lean `ring_racy_kernel` (small size); MojoProofTest/GPUProof/Chunks.lean (`ring11_racy_chunks`, `ring37_racy_chunks`, two `#guard_msgs`); MojoProofTest/RacySig.lean (`vadd_sig_racy`); MojoProofTest/RacySigRun.lean (`trSig_run`, `vadd_run_racy`); the toy tests of `racy`, `hb`, `ordered` (MojoProofTest/GPUProof/Toy, Race, Clean, Laminar, Uses) unchanged; logs/s46b-prof-racysig.log; MojoProofTest/GPUProof/Race.lean `aliased_Racy`; logs/s48b-vadd-before.log, s48b-vadd-after.log, s48b-vadd1-after.log; scratch tmp/s50b/RK.lean, RP.lean, logs/s50b-rk1.log, s50b-rk2.log, s50b-rp1.log |
